The reverse inequality in the above Lemma is not always true. For when they are true, we refer the reader to
[
56
, Theorem 1.5]
and
[
18
, Theorem 7.2]
.

The following proposition is a slight modification of
[
3
, Proposition 4.7]
, whose proof can be found in the Appendix. It has the advantage that the absolute value sign inside the norm is removed.

Proposition 3.17
.

Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
p\in\mathbb{\mathbb{N}}
is odd and
p>2-n
. Then for any
\alpha\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{f^{2p+n-2}}_{1,\alpha}\leq 2\frac{2p+n-2}{p}\norm{f^{2(p+n - 2)}}_{L^{1}_{M}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\norm{f^p}_{2,2\alpha}^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.9)

whenever the right hand side is finite. In particular,

\displaystyle\norm{f^{2p+n - 2}}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}\leq\sqrt{8}\frac{2p+n-2}{p}\norm{f^{2(p+n-2)}}_{L^{1}_{M}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}\left(f^{p},f^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

(3.10)

whenever the right hand side is finite.

Recall in the Euclidean setting, many useful inequalities that connects the Besov norms and Sobelev norms are results of Berstein-type inequality. Under the current setting, it is unclear how one might obtain a Berstein-type inequality, since the energy measure who plays the role of derivative is singular with respect to the reference measure
\mu
. Nonetheless, one can still make connections between the Besov and Sobelev norm via the following proposition.

Proposition 3.18
.

Suppose
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and
p\in\mathbb{\mathbb{N}}
is odd and
p\geq 2-n
. Then
we have

\norm{f^{n+2p-2}}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{d_{w}/2}_{1,\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f^{2p+2n-4}}_{L^{1}_{M}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}(f^{p},f^{p})^{\frac{1}{2}},

whenever the right hand side is finite.

Proof.

Let
p,\,n\in\mathbb{N}
and assume
p
is an odd number and
p\geq n-2
, then by Lemma
3.15
and Proposition
3.17
, we see

\displaystyle\norm{f^{2p+n - 2}}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}_{1,\infty}^{\frac{d_{w}}{2}}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq: compare our besov and baudoin besov}}{\lesssim}\norm{f^{2p+n-2}}_{1,\frac{1}{2}}\overset{\eqref{ineq: mod of baudoin berstein and d form}}{\lesssim}\norm{f^{2(p+n - 2)}}_{L^{1}_{M}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{E}\left(f^{p},f^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}},

which is the desired result.
∎

The following one direction relation between Besov spaces and Sobelev spaces is useful.

Lemma 3.19
.

\mathcal{B}^{\frac{d_{w}}{2}}_{2,\infty}\subset\mathcal{F}\cap L^{2}(M,m)
and

\displaystyle\norm{f}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{\frac{d_{w}}{2}}_{2,\infty}}\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}(f,f)}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.15
and
[
3
, Proposition 4.6]
, we see

\displaystyle\norm{f}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}_{2,\infty}^{\frac{d_{w}}{2}}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{2,\frac{d_{w}}{2}}\lesssim\sqrt{\mathcal{E}(f,f)}.

∎

The following lemma is a well-known result for strongly local Dirichlet forms, which can be found in
[
52
, Lemma 3.2.5 and Theorem 3.2.2]
.

Proposition 3.20
.

For
p\geq 2
, if
f\in\mathcal{F}\cap\,\mathcal{C}_{M}
, then
f^{p}\in\mathcal{F}
, and

\mathcal{E}(f^{p},f^{p})=(p-1)^{2}\int_{M}f^{2(p-1)}d\Gamma(f,f).

We also have the following Hardy-Littlewood-Sobolev inequality.

Lemma 3.21
.

Suppose
M
is compact and the heat kernel satisfies
p_{t}(x,y)\lesssim t^{-\beta/2}
for all
t\in(0,1],x,y\in M
with
\beta\geq 2
.

•

If
\beta=2
, then for every
1\leq q<+\infty
, we have for all
f\in\mathcal{F}

\norm{f}_{L^{q}(\mu)}\lesssim\mathcal{E}(f,f)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\norm{f}_{L^{2}_{M}}.

•

For
\beta>2
, the above estimate holds for all
q\leq q^{\prime}=\frac{2\beta}{\beta-2}
.

Proof.

The second item follows from
[
110
]
, the Nash inequality, and the compactness hypothesis. See also
[
3
, Theorem 1.1]
for a more general result. The first item follows from the second item by applying the obvious inequality
t^{-1}\leq t^{-\frac{2+\varepsilon}{2}}
for all
t\in(0,1]
and then making
\varepsilon
small enough so that
q\leq\frac{4}{\varepsilon}<\frac{2(2+\varepsilon)}{\varepsilon}
for any arbitrarily large
q
.
∎

4
Multiplication of distributions and Schauder estimates

4.1
Multiplicative inequality

In this section, we construct the product decomposition and prove Theorem
1.7
.
Our overall strategy follows
[
10
, Section 3]
, but the singularity of the energy measure in our setting forces certain modifications. We discuss these first, both to motivate our choice of product decomposition and to indicate where new ingredients are required.

When the heat kernel satisfies (

HKE
f

) with
d_{w}=2
and
\Theta=1
in (

HC
Θ

) — the framework of
[
10
]
— it is classical that
\mathcal{D}(L)
forms an algebra and the energy measure formally defined as

\Gamma(f,g):=-\tfrac{1}{2}\bigl(L(fg)-fL(g)-gL(f)\bigr),\qquad f,g\in\mathcal{D}(L),

is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu
, with Radon–Nikodym density, i.e. the
carré du champ operator
. In that regime, the product estimates of
[
10
]
rest on a point-wise bound of the form

\displaystyle t\,\Gamma(P_{t}f,P_{t}f)(x)\;\lesssim\;\int_{M}k_{t}(x,y)\,|f(y)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}y),

(4.1)

where
k_{t}
satisfies Gaussian upper estimates.

Under Assumption
1.1
,
d_{w}
may exceed
2
and
\Gamma(f,f)
need not be absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu
, so (
4.1
) is no longer available as a starting point. In fact, by
[
79
]
,
d_{w}>2
implies
\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f,f)
is singular with respect to the reference measure
\mu
on
M
. We instead exploit a feature internal to the decomposition: in the resonant term (
4.5
), every occurrence of
\Gamma
is further composed with
P
- and
Q
-operators. This additional smoothing — which plays no role in the argument of
[
10
]
— already suffices to control the
resonant term directly at the level of the measure
\Gamma(P_{t}f,P_{t}f)
via a Caccioppoli-type estimate (Theorem
4.5
), bypassing the need for a point-wise carré du champ operator.

The following results will be used to justify the agreement of our product decomposition with point-wise multiplication.

Lemma 4.1
.

For any
g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{C}_{M}
,
t>0
and
n=0,1,2,\dots
, we have the following point-wise identity,

\displaystyle t\mathcal{E}(q_{n,t}(x,\cdot),g)=Q^{(n+1)}_{t}g(x),\qquad x\in M.

(4.2)

Proof.

Recall the formula

\displaystyle 2\mathcal{E}(f,g)=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\iint_{M^{2}}\left(f(y)-f(z)\right)\left(g(y)-g(z)\right)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

(4.3)

Now consider by Fubini’s and semi-group properties that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\iint_{M^{2}}\left(q_{n,t}(x,y)-q_{n,t}(x,z)\right)\left(g(y)-g(z)\right)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\iint_{M^{2}}q_{n,t}(x,y)g(y)-q_{n,t}(x,y)g(z)-q_{n,t}(x,z)g(y)+q_{n,t}(x,z)g(z)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{-1}\iint_{M^{2}}q_{n,t}(x,y)g(y)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)-\iint_{M^{2}}q_{n,t}(x,y)g(z)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)

\displaystyle\qquad+\varepsilon^{-1}\iint_{M^{2}}q_{n,t}(x,z)g(z)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)-\iint_{M^{2}}q_{n,t}(x,z)g(y)p_{\varepsilon}(y,z)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\varepsilon^{-1}\left((tL)^{n}e^{-tL}g(x)-e^{-\varepsilon L}(tL)^{n}e^{-tL}g(x)\right).

Sending
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
to see the above expression converges in sup norm to
LQ^{(n)}_{t}g(x)
. Therefore, for
x\in M
,

\displaystyle t\mathcal{E}(q_{n,t}(x,\cdot),g)

\displaystyle=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\varepsilon^{-1}\left((tL)^{n}e^{-tL}g(x)-e^{-\varepsilon L}(tL)^{n}e^{-tL}g(x)\right)

\displaystyle=tLQ^{(n)}_{t}g(x)

\displaystyle=Q^{(n+1)}_{t}g(x),

which is the desired result.
∎

The following corollary is a direct consequence of Lemma
4.1
and (
2.2
).

Corollary 4.2
.

For
f,g\in\mathcal{F}\cap\mathcal{C}_{M}
and
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, the following point-wise in
(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times M
,

\displaystyle Q^{(k)}_{t}\Gamma(f,g)(x):=\int_{M}q_{k,t}(x,\cdot)\mathrm{d}\Gamma(f,g)=\mathcal{E}(fq_{k,t}(x,\cdot),g)+\mathcal{E}\left(gq_{k,t}(x,\cdot),f\right)-\mathcal{E}(fg,q_{k,t}(x,\cdot)).

Furthermore, if in addition
f,g\in\mathcal{D}_{2}(L)
, then

\displaystyle Q^{(k)}_{t}\Gamma\left(f,g\right)(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}fq_{k,t}(x,\cdot)\left(Lg\right)\mathrm{d}\mu+\int_{M}gq_{k,t}(x,\cdot)\left(Lf\right)\mathrm{d}\mu-t^{-1}Q_{t}^{(k+1)}\left(f\cdot g\right)(x)

\displaystyle=Q^{(k)}_{t}\left(f\cdot\left(Lg\right)\right)(x)+Q^{(k)}_{t}\left(g\cdot\left(Lf\right)\right)(x)-t^{-1}Q_{t}^{(k+1)}\left(f\cdot g\right)(x).

4.1.1
Product Decomposition

We follow strictly the decomposition in
[
10
]
. Given two functions
f,g\in\mathcal{C}_{M}
, denote

\displaystyle\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g):=P_{1}^{(b)}\left(P_{1}^{(b)}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{1}g\right).

By the Calderon’s reproducing formula (Theorem
3.1
), the following equality holds in
\mathcal{C}_{M}
for
b\in\mathbb{N}
:

\displaystyle fg

\displaystyle=\lim_{t\to 0}P^{(b)}_{t}\left(P^{(b)}_{t}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)=-\int_{0}^{1}\partial_{t}\left(P^{(b)}_{t}\left(P^{(b)}_{t}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+\Delta_{-1}(f,g)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}P_{t}^{(b)}\left(Q^{(b)}_{t}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)+P^{(b)}_{t}\left(P^{(b)}_{t}f\cdot Q^{(b)}_{t}g\right)+Q^{(b)}_{t}\left(P_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\Delta_{-1}(f,g)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}(1)+(2)+(3)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}\left(f,g\right).

By Corollary
4.2
, the following equality holds point-wise:

\displaystyle(3)=Q^{(b)}_{t}\left(P_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)

\displaystyle=Q^{(b-1)}_{t}\left((tL)P_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)+Q^{(b-1)}_{t}\left(P_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot(tL)P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad-2tQ^{(b-1)}_{t}\Gamma\left(P_{t}^{(b)}f,P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)

\displaystyle:=B_{g}^{(b)}(f)+B_{f}^{(b)}(g)+R^{(b)}(f,g).

Thus, we can decompose the product by

\displaystyle fg=\int_{0}^{1}\left\{(1)+B_{g}^{(b)}(f)\right\}+\left\{(2)+B_{f}^{(b)}(g)\right\}+R^{(b)}(f,g)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g).

By Corollary
4.2
again, we have the point-wise identity

\displaystyle(1)=P^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)

\displaystyle=P_{t}^{(b)}\left(tLQ^{(b-1)}_{t}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)

\displaystyle=\left\{2P^{(b)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f,P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)-P^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f\cdot tL\left(P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)\right)\right\}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad+tLP^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)

\displaystyle:=S^{(b)}(f,g)+A_{g}^{(b)}(f)

where
S^{(b)}(f,g)
is the difference in brackets. Similarly,

\displaystyle(2)=A_{f}^{(b)}(g)+S^{(b)}(g,f).

Definition 4.3
.

Given an integer
b\geq 2
and
f,g\in\mathcal{C}_{M}
, we define their
para-product
, parametrized by
b\in\mathbb{N}
, by the formula

\displaystyle\Pi_{g}^{(b)}(f)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{t}\left\{A_{g}^{(b)}(f)+B_{g}^{(b)}(f)\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}tLP_{t}^{(b)}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f\cdot P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)+Q^{(b-1)}_{t}\left(tLP_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}.

We will prove the well-definition of the para-product by bounding its operator norm later. With this notation, Calderon’s reproducing formula for
f,g\in\mathcal{C}_{M}
becomes the point-wise identity

fg=\Pi_{g}^{(b)}(f)+\Pi_{f}^{(b)}(g)+\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)+\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g),

(4.4)

with the “low-frequency tail”

\displaystyle\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g):=P_{1}^{(b)}\left(P^{(1)}_{1}f\cdot P_{1}^{(b)}g\right)

and the “resonant term”

\displaystyle\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{S(f,g)+S(g,f)+R(f,g)\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

(4.5)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{2P^{(b)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f,P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)-P^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f\cdot tL\left(P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)\right)\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{2P^{(b)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}g,P_{t}^{(b)}f\right)-P^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}g\cdot(tL)\left(P_{t}^{(b)}f\right)\right)\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}Q^{(b-1)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(P^{(b)}_{t}f,P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}.

4.1.2
Energy measure and Besov norms

In this subsection, we state a relation between energy measures and Besov norms, which will be essential for controlling the resonant term.

We will need the following version of the Cacciopoli inequality, whose proof can be found in the Appendix.

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists
C_{s}>0
so that for all
r>0
and ball
B
of radius
r
and
f\in\mathcal{D}_{2}(L)
,

\displaystyle\Gamma(f,f)\left(B\right)\leq r^{d_{w}}\norm{Lf}_{L^{2}_{B}}^{2}+\frac{C_{s}}{r^{d_{w}}}\mathrm{osc}^{2}_{B}(f),

where

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}^{2}_{B}\left(f\right):=\norm{\frac{1}{\mu(B)} \int_B f(\cdot) - f(y)\mu(\mathrm dy) }_{L^{2}_{B}}^{2}.

Theorem 4.5
.

Assume
d_{h}+2\Theta-d_{w}>0
. Suppose
\alpha<\Theta
and
U,V\in\{Q^{(k)}:k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\}
.
Then it holds uniformly in
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}
that

\displaystyle t\,\norm{V_t \Gamma(U_t f, U_t f)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\lesssim t^{\frac{2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}.

Proof.

For simplicity, we will assume
U_{t}=V_{t}=P_{t}
for
t>0
. The proof for the other cases are verbatim. For
(t,x)\in(0,\infty)\times M
and
j\in\mathbb{N}
, denote
B^{t,x}_{j}:=B(x,2^{-j}t^{\frac{1}{d_{w}}})
.

Recall
\Phi
in Assumption
1.1
, by Lemma
4.4
, we have

\displaystyle tP_{t}\Gamma\left(P_{t}f,P_{t}f\right)(x)

\displaystyle=t\int_{M}p_{t}(x,y)\Gamma\left(P_{t}f,P_{t}f\right)(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle=t\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{B^{t,x}_{j}\backslash B^{t,x}_{j+1}}p_{t}(x,y)\Gamma\left(P_{t}f,P_{t}f\right)(\mathrm{d}y)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:sgu}}{\lesssim}t\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)\Gamma\left(P_{t}f,P_{t}f\right)\left(B^{t,x}_{j}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim t\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\left(2^{-j}t^{\frac{1}{d_{w}}}\right)^{d_{w}}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)\norm{LP_tf}_{L^{2}_{B_{j}^{t,x}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+t\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\left(2^{-j}t^{\frac{1}{d_{w}}}\right)^{-d_{w}}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)\mathrm{osc}_{B^{t,x}_{j}}^{2}\left(P_{t}f\right)

\displaystyle=:\mathrm{I}+\mathrm{II}.

For I, it holds uniformly in
x\in M
and
t\in(0,1]
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}

\displaystyle=t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}2^{-jd_{w}}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)\norm{Q^{(1)}_tf}_{L^{2}_{B^{t,x}_{j}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}2^{-jd_{w}}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)\mu\left(B^{t,x}_{j}\right)\norm{Q^{(1)}_tf}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:a_reg}}{\lesssim}t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)2^{-jd_{w}}2^{-jd_{h}}t^{\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}.

For
g\in\mathcal{C}_{M}
and an open set
B\subset M
, define

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}^{\infty}_{B}(g):=\sup_{x,y\in B}\absolutevalue{f(x) - f(y)}.

Then by Hölder,

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}_{B}^{2}(P_{t}f)\leq\mu(B)\mathrm{osc}_{B}^{\infty}(P_{t}f)^{2}.

By Calderon’s reproducing formula, we thus have for
j\geq 0

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}_{B_{j}^{t,x}}^{2}\left(P_{t}f\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:a_reg}}{\lesssim}2^{-jd_{h}}t^{\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\mathrm{osc}_{B_{j}^{t,x}}^{\infty}\left(P_{t}f\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2^{-jd_{h}}t^{\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\left(\mathrm{osc}_{B^{t,x}_{j}}^{\infty}\left(P_{2}f\right)+\int_{t}^{2}\mathrm{osc}_{B^{t,x}_{j}}^{\infty}\left(Q^{(1)}_{s}f\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:holder_kernel}}{\lesssim}2^{-jd_{h}}t^{\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\left(\left(2^{-j\Theta}t^{\frac{\Theta}{d_{w}}}\right)\norm{P_1f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}+\int_{t}^{2}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)^{\frac{\Theta}{d_{w}}}2^{-j\Theta}\norm{Q^{(1)}_{s/2}f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j(d_{h}+2\Theta)}t^{\frac{2\Theta+d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\left(\norm{P_1f}+\int_{t}^{2}s^{\frac{-\Theta+\alpha}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}\norm{f}_{\dot{\mathcal{B}}_{\infty}^{\alpha}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim 2^{-j(d_{h}+2\Theta)}t^{\frac{2\Theta+d_{h}}{d_{w}}}t^{\frac{2\alpha-2\Theta}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle=2^{-j(d_{h}+2\Theta)}t^{\frac{d_{h}+2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2},

where we used the fact that
\alpha<\Theta
and
t\in(0,1]
in the fifth line. Similarly, for
j\leq 0
we decompose the integral as

\displaystyle\int_{t}^{2}\mathrm{osc}_{B_{j}^{t,x}}^{\infty}\left(Q_{s}^{(1)}f\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}

\displaystyle=\left(\int_{t}^{2^{-j}t^{1/d_{w}}}+\int_{2^{-j}t^{1/d_{w}}}^{2}\right)\mathrm{osc}_{B_{j}^{t,x}}\left(Q^{(1)}_{s}f\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{t}^{2^{-j}t^{1/d_{w}}}2^{-j\Theta}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)^{\frac{\Theta}{d_{w}}}s^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}+\int_{2^{-j}t^{1/d_{w}}}^{2}2^{-j\Theta}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)^{\frac{\Theta}{d_{w}}}s^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}

\displaystyle=\int_{t}^{2}2^{-j\Theta}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)^{\frac{\Theta}{d_{w}}}s^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

Hence, for
j\leq-1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{osc}_{B_{j}^{t,x}}^{2}\left(P_{t}f\right)\lesssim 2^{-j\left(d_{h}+2\Theta\right)}t^{\frac{d_{h}+2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}.

Insert this into II to see

\displaystyle\mathrm{II}

\displaystyle\lesssim t\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}t^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}-1}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)2^{j(d_{h}+2\Theta-d_{w})}t^{\frac{d_{h}+2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}

\displaystyle=t^{\frac{2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2}\sum_{j=-\infty}^{\infty}\Phi\left(2^{-j}\right)2^{-j(d_{h}+2\Theta-d_{w})}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{2\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}^{2},

where we used the assumption
d_{h}+2\Theta-d_{w}>0
in the last line. Collect both estimates for the desired result.
∎

Note that the proof of
4.5
requires us to control the oscillation of
Q^{(k)}f
uniformly on small balls, which depends on
\Theta
. This is the source of the
\Theta
hypothesis in Theorem
1.7
.

4.1.3
Product estimates

The goal here is to prove Theorem
1.7
. We will estimate each piece of the decomposition (
4.4
), which when collected will imply the inequality in Theorem
1.7
.

Proposition 4.6
.

Let
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, for every positive
\gamma
and
b\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds uniformly for
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty},g\in\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}
that

\displaystyle\norm{\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Proof.

Let
a\geq\gamma
and consider for
s\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle Q^{(a)}_{s}\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g)=Q^{(a)}_{s}P_{1}^{(b)}\left(P_{1}^{(b)}f\cdot P^{(b)}_{1}g\right).

Observe by Lemma
3.5
that
P_{1}^{(b)}f\in L^{\infty}_{M}
, and
Q^{(a)}_{s}P_{1}^{(b)}=s^{a}e^{-sL}L^{a}P_{1}^{(b)}
. Hence we obtain by Proposition
3.3
that

\displaystyle\norm{Q_s^{(b)} \Delta_{-1}(f,g)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle=s^{a}\norm{e^{-sL}L^aP_1^{(b)} \left(P^{(b)}_1f \cdot P^{(b)}_1 g \right)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{a}\norm{L^a P_1^{(b)}}_{L^{\infty}_{M}\to L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{P_1^{(b)} f\cdot P_1^{(b)} g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{a}\norm{P_1^{(b)} f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{P_1^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{\gamma}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Where we used the fact
s\in(0,1]
and
a\geq\gamma
in the last inequality.
∎

Proposition 4.7
.

Let
b\in\mathbb{N}
with
b\geq 2
, suppose
\alpha\in(-d_{w},d_{w})
,

•

Uniformly in
g\in L^{\infty}_{M}
and
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

•

Uniformly in
g\in\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}
and
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}
with
\beta<0
and
\alpha+\beta\in(-d_{w},d_{w})
,

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha+\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\Pi_{g}^{(b)}(f)=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}tLP_{t}^{(b)}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f\cdot P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)+Q^{(b-1)}_{t}\left(tLP_{t}^{(b)}f\cdot P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}.

Consider
Q^{(b-1)}_{s}\Pi_{g}^{(b)}(f)
, and split the above integral into two intervals
(0,s)
and
[s,1]
. For
s\leq t
, we use

\displaystyle Q^{(b-1)}_{s}(tL)P_{t}^{(b)}=\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}(tL)^{b}P_{t}^{(b)}e^{-sL};\quad Q^{(b-1)}_{s}Q^{(b-1)}_{t}=\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}Q^{2(b-1)}_{t}e^{-sL}.

For
t\leq s
, we use

\displaystyle Q^{(b-1)}_{s}(tL)P_{t}^{(b)}=\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)Q^{(b)}_{s}P_{t}^{(b)};\quad Q_{s}^{(b-1)}Q_{t}^{(b-1)}=\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)Q^{(b)}_{s}Q^{(b-2)}_{t}.

By the boundedness in
L^{\infty}_{M}\to L^{\infty}_{M}
norm of
Q,P
operators, we have

\displaystyle\norm{Q_s^{(b - 1)}\Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)\left(\norm{Q_t^{(b - 1)}f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{P_t^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}+\norm{tL P_t^{(b)} f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{P_t^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

(4.6)

\displaystyle+\int_{s}^{1}\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}\left(\norm{Q_t^{(b - 1)}f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{P_t^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}+\norm{tL P_t^{(b)} f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{P_t^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}.

Since
0<\alpha<d_{w}
and
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}
, Proposition
3.2
gives us

\displaystyle\norm{Q_t^{(b - 1)} f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}+\norm{(tL)^1 P_t^{(b)}f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\lesssim t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

(4.7)

For
g\in L^{\infty}_{M}
,

\displaystyle\norm{P_t^{(b)} g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}},\quad\text{ uniformly in }t\in(0,1].

(4.8)

If
g\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty}
for some
\beta<0
, then

\displaystyle\norm{P_t^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{t}^{1}\norm{Q^{(b)}_u g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}+\norm{P_1^{(b)}g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

(4.9)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{t}^{1}u^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}+1\right)\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Therefore, if
g\in L^{\infty}_{M}
, since
\alpha>-d_{w}
, we may insert (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) into (
4.6
) to see

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(b- 1)}_t \Pi_g^{(b)}(f)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+\int_{s}^{1}\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle=\left(s^{-1}\int_{0}^{s}t^{1+\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+s^{b-1}\int_{s}^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}-(b-1)}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(s^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}+s^{b-1}+s^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}},

where we used the fact that
1+\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}>0
in the third line, and the fact that
b-1\geq\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}
for
b\geq 2
,
1\geq\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}
in the last line.

If
g\in{\mathcal{B}}^{\beta}_{\infty}
with
\alpha+\beta\in(-d_{w},d_{w})
, we see by inserting (
4.7
) and (
4.9
) into (
4.6
), we see that

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(b - 1)}_s \Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{s}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)^{1}t^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+\int_{s}^{1}\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}t^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(s^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}+s^{b-1}+s^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

This holds since
b-1\geq 1>\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}>-1
. This holds uniformly for
s\in(0,1]
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.8
.

Suppose
b\in\mathbb{N}
, then for any
\alpha,\beta\in(-\infty,\Theta)
with
\alpha+\beta>0
, the following inequality holds uniformly in
f\in\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}
and
g\in\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}
,

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha+\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Proof.

Recall

\displaystyle\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{2P^{(b)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f,P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)-P^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}f\cdot tL\left(P_{t}^{(b)}g\right)\right)\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}\left\{2P^{(b)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}g,P_{t}^{(b)}f\right)-P^{(b)}_{t}\left(Q_{t}^{(b-1)}g\cdot tL\left(P_{t}^{(b)}f\right)\right)\right\}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{2}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{1}Q^{(b-1)}_{t}t\Gamma\left(P^{(b)}_{t}f,P^{(b)}_{t}g\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}.

For
s\in(0,1]
we consider
Q^{(b-1)}_{s}\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)
. We again split the integrals on
[0,1]
into
[0,s]
and
[s,1]
and call them
\mathrm{I}
and
\mathrm{II}
respectively.

First consider
\mathrm{I}
, the case
t\leq s
. We see that

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(b - 1)}_tf}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}+\norm{tL P_t^{(b)}f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\lesssim t^{\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

For terms that involve
\Gamma
, since
\max\{\alpha,\beta\}<\Theta
, we see by Theorem
4.5
, that for
U_{t},V_{t},W_{t}\in\{Q^{(b-1)}_{t},P_{t}^{(b)},tLP_{t}^{(b)}\}
that

\displaystyle\norm{tV_t \Gamma\left(U_t f,W_t g\right)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:cs for energy measure}}{\leq}\norm{\pr{t\int_M \abs{V}_t(\cdot, y) \Gamma\pr{U_tf,U_tf }(\mathrm d y)}^\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{\pr{t\int_M \abs{V}_t(\cdot, y) \Gamma\pr{W_tg,W_tg }(\mathrm d y)}^\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}},

where
\absolutevalue{V}_{t}(x,y)
denotes the absolute value of the integral kernel of the operator
V_{t}
with respect to
\mu
.
Indeed, the proof of Theorem
4.5
requires only the upper bound in (

HKE
f

).
Hence, for
0\leq t\leq s\leq 1

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(b - 1)}_s (\mathrm I)}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}t^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Where we used the fact that
\alpha+\beta>0
.

For the second part, we observe that for
t>s
,

\displaystyle Q^{(b-1)}_{s}P_{t}^{(b)}=\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}e^{-sL}(tL)^{b-1}P_{t}^{(b)};\quad Q^{(b-1)}_{s}Q^{(b-1)}_{t}=\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}Q^{2(b-1)}_{t}e^{-sL}.

Thus we get for the second part

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(b - 1)}_s (\mathrm{\Rnum{2}})}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{s}^{1}\left(\frac{s}{t}\right)^{b-1}t^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(s^{b-1}+s^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\right)\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim s^{\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}},

where we used the fact that
b-1>1\geq\frac{2\Theta}{d_{w}}>\frac{\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}
and
s\in(0,1]
.
∎

The proof of the following elementary lemma can be found in the Appendix.

Lemma 4.9
.

Let
u\in{\mathcal{S}}
and
\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, the following inequalities hold,

\displaystyle\norm{u}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\norm{u}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}},\qquad\text{ for }\alpha\leq\beta

\displaystyle\norm{u}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\norm{u}_{L^{\infty}_{M}},\qquad\text{ for }\alpha\leq 0.

\displaystyle\norm{u}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\norm{u}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}},\qquad\text{ for }\alpha>0.

Proof of Theorem
1.7
.

Assume
\alpha>\beta
are real numbers so that
\alpha>0
,
\beta\in\left(-\Theta,\Theta\right)
and
\alpha+\beta>0
. Then there exists
\sigma\in(0,\Theta)
so that
\sigma+\beta>0
, Set
\gamma:=\min\{\alpha,\sigma\}\in(0,\Theta)
.

Consider for sufficiently large
b\in\mathbb{N}
that

\displaystyle\norm{f \cdot g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\leq\norm{\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}+\norm{\Pi^{(b)}_f(g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}+\norm{\Pi_g^{(b)}(f)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}+\norm{\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

By Proposition
4.6
we see

\norm{\Delta_{-1}^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Since
\beta<\Theta
, and
\alpha>0
, we see by Proposition
4.7
and Lemma
4.9
that

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi_f^{(b)}(g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\cdot\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Similarly, since
\gamma<\Theta
, we see if
\beta>0
, it holds by Proposition
4.7
and Lemma
4.9
that

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{L^{\infty}_{M}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

On the other hand, if
\beta<0
, we see

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{\Pi^{(b)}_g(f)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma+\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}.

Finally, since
\gamma+\beta>0
, we see from Lemma
4.9
and Proposition
4.8
that

\displaystyle\norm{\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{\Pi^{(b)}(f,g)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta+\gamma}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{f}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{\infty}}\norm{g}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}}.

Collecting all terms to see the desired inequality.
∎

4.2
Schauder estimate

In this section, we prove a Schauder-type estimate. For any normed space
(\mathcal{B},\norm{\cdot}_{\mathcal{B}})
, we denote by
\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\alpha}\mathcal{B}
the space of
\alpha
-Hölder continuous functions from
[0,T]
to
\mathcal{B}
, equipped with its natural norm. We write
\mathcal{C}_{T}\mathcal{B}:=\mathcal{C}_{T}^{0}\mathcal{B}
.
We define the
resolution operator

{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
via the formula

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}(v)_{t}:=\int_{0}^{t}P^{(k)}_{t-s}v(s)\mathrm{d}s;

(4.10)

Proposition 4.10

(Schauder Estimate)
.

Let
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
p\in[1,\infty]
and
T>0
be arbitrary but fixed. if
v\in{\mathcal{C}}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}
,
{\mathcal{R}}^{(k)}(v)
belongs to
{\mathcal{C}}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{\beta+d_{w}}_{p,\infty}
. Furthermore, for all
\eta\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{{\mathcal R}^{(k)}(v)_t}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta+d_{w}/\eta}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim(t+t^{\frac{\eta-1}{\eta}})\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}},\quad\text{ for all }t\in[0,T].

Moreover, if
\alpha\in\mathbb{R}
with
-d_{w}<\beta-\alpha<0
, then

\displaystyle\norm{{\mathcal R}^{(k)}(v)}_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,\infty}}\lesssim\norm{v}_{{\mathcal{C}}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

Proof.

We fix
b\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, and denote
\mathcal{R}:=\mathcal{R}^{(b)}
for notational convenience. Let
c\geq\frac{|\beta|}{d_{w}}+1
be an integer and
\tau\in(0,1]
. Then

\displaystyle Q^{(c)}_{\tau}{\mathcal{R}}(v)_{t}=\int_{0}^{t}Q^{(c)}_{\tau}P^{(b)}_{t-s}v(s)ds.

For
0<t-s
and
\tau>0
, we have

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau P^{(b)}_{t-s} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}=\norm{P^{(b)}_{t-s}Q^{(c)}_\tau v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\leq\norm{P_{t-s}^{(b)}}_{L^{p}_{M}\to L^{p}_{M}}\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\lesssim\tau^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

(4.11)

But for
0<t-s\leq\tau
, we see that
\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}<1
and hence

\displaystyle\tau^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}=\left(t-s+\tau\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\lesssim\left(t-s+\tau\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}\right)^{c}.

Thus

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau P^{(b)}_{t-s} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq: shauder step 1}}{\lesssim}\tau^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}

(4.12)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\tau}{\tau+t-s}\right)^{c}\left(\tau+t-s\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

For the case
t-s>\tau
,

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau P^{(b)}_{t-s} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s}\right)^{c}\norm{P_{\tau}(t-s)^c L^c P^{(b)}_{t-s}v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s}\right)^{c}\norm{(t-s)^c L^c P_{t-s}^{(c)} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s}\right)^{c}\sum_{k=0}^{c-1}\frac{1}{k!}\norm{Q^{(k+c)}_{t-s}v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s}\right)^{c}\sum_{k=0}^{c-1}\frac{1}{k!}(t-s)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\sup_{\tau\in(0,t-s]}\tau^{-\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q_{\tau}^{(k+c)} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s}\right)^{c}(t-s)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

Note for
t-s\geq\tau
implies
\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}\geq\frac{1}{2}\frac{\tau}{t-s}
, and

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\left(t-s\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\leq\left(t-s+\tau\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}},&\beta\geq 0\\
(t-s)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}=2^{-\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\left(2(t-s)\right)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\lesssim(t-s+\tau)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}},&\beta\leq 0\end{cases}

Hence for
t-s\geq\tau
, it holds that

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau P_{t-s}^{(b)} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\lesssim\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}(t-s+\tau)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

(4.13)

Combining (
4.12
) and (
4.13
), we see that for
t-s>0
and
\tau\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau P_{t-s}^{(b)} v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\lesssim\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}\right)^{c}(t-s+\tau)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

Thus for
\eta\geq 1
, and
\eta^{\prime}=\frac{\eta}{\eta-1}
(assume
1/0=\infty
), we may choose
c
large so that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau P^{(b)}_{t-s}v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}ds

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}\left(\frac{\tau}{t-s+\tau}\right)^{c}(t-s+\tau)^{\frac{\beta}{d_{w}}}ds\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq t^{\frac{1}{\eta^{\prime}}}\left(\int_{0}^{t}\tau^{c\eta}(t-s+\tau)^{\frac{\beta\eta}{d_{w}}-\eta c}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\lesssim t^{\frac{1}{\eta^{\prime}}}\left(\tau^{c\eta}(t+\tau)^{\frac{\eta\beta+d_{w}}{d_{w}}-c\eta}+\tau^{\frac{\beta\eta+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}\right)^{\frac{1}{\eta}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq t^{\frac{1}{\eta^{\prime}}}\tau^{\frac{\beta+d_{w}/\eta}{d_{w}}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}},

where we used the fact that for large
c
,
\frac{\beta\eta+d_{w}}{d_{w}}-c\eta<0
implies
(t+\tau)^{\frac{\beta\eta+d_{w}}{d_{w}}-c\eta}\leq\tau^{\frac{\beta\eta+d_{w}}{d_{w}}-c}
in the last inequality. Hence

\displaystyle\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\tau^{-\frac{\beta+d_{w}/\eta}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q^{(c)}_\tau{\mathcal R}(v)_t}_{L^{p}_{M}}\lesssim t^{\frac{1}{\eta^{\prime}}}\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

Also,

\displaystyle\norm{P_1 {\mathcal R}(v)_t}_{L^{p}_{M}}\leq\int_{0}^{t}\norm{P^{(b)}_{t-s} P_1v(s)}_{L^{p}_{M}}ds\lesssim T\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

This concludes the proof of the first part of the proposition.

For the second part, note that for for
0\leq s<t\leq T
,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(v)_{t}-{\mathcal{R}}(v)_{s}=\int_{0}^{s}\left(P_{t-r}^{(b)}-P_{s-r}^{(b)}\right)v(r)dr+\int_{s}^{t}P^{(b)}_{t-r}v(r)dr

(4.14)

Observe also

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\left(P_{t-r}^{(b)}-P_{s-r}^{(b)}\right)v(r)dr

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}\partial_{z}P^{(b)}_{z-r}dzv(r)dr

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\gamma_{b}}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}Q^{(b)}_{z-r}\frac{dz}{z-r}v(r)dr.

Hence, by Lemma
3.5
, we have for
\alpha-\beta>0

and sufficiently large
k\in\mathbb{N}
, it holds for
h\in(0,1]
that

\displaystyle h^{-\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q^{(k)}_h\int_0^s \left(P^{(b)}_{t-r} - P^{(b)}_{s-r} \right) v(r)dr}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}h^{-\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q_h^{(k)}Q_{z-r}^{(b)} v(r)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\frac{dz}{z-r}dr

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{s\in[0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}(z-r)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}-1}dz\,dr.

Since
\alpha-\beta>0

and
\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}>-1
, we see

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}(z-r)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}-1}dz\,dr

\displaystyle=\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}\int_{0}^{s}(t-r)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}}-(s-r)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}}dr

\displaystyle\lesssim\left|t^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}-s^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}\right|+(t-s)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(t-s)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}},

where the last inequality is due to the fact that
\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}\in(0,1]
.
Hence

\displaystyle h^{-\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q^{(k)}_h\int_0^s \left(P^{(b)}_{t-r} - P^{(b)}_{s-r} \right) v(r)dr}_{L^{p}_{M}}\lesssim\left(t-s\right)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}\sup_{s\in(0,t]}\norm{v(s)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}

(4.15)

Finally, we see again by Lemma
3.5
and the fact that
-d_{w}<\beta-\alpha<0
that

\displaystyle\int_{s}^{t}h^{-\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q^{(k)}_h P_{t-r}^{(b)} v(r)}_{L^{p}_{M}}dr

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{s}^{t}\sum_{\ell=0}^{b}h^{-\frac{\alpha}{d_{w}}}\norm{Q^{(k)}_h Q^{(\ell)}_{t-r} v(r)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\mathrm{d}r

(4.16)

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\norm{v(r)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}\int_{s}^{t}(t-r)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha}{d_{w}}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\norm{v(r)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}(t-s)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}.

Plug (
4.15
) and (
4.16
) into (
4.14
) to see for sufficiently large
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\sup_{\tau\in(0,1]}\norm{Q^{(k)}_\tau\pr{ \mathcal R(v)_t -\mathcal R(v)_s}}_{L^{p}_{M}}}{\left(t-s\right)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}}\lesssim\norm{v}_{\mathcal{C}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

(4.17)

Now observe that

\displaystyle\norm{P_1 \int_0^s \pr{P_{t-s}^{(b)} - P_{s-t}^{(b)}} v(r) \mathrm ds}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}\norm{Q^{(b)}_{z-r} P_1 v(r)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{z-r}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}\norm{Q_{z-r}^{(b)}}_{L^{p}_{M}\to L^{p}_{M}}\norm{P_1v(r)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\frac{\mathrm{d}z}{z-r}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{0}^{s}\int_{s}^{t}(z-r)^{-1}\mathrm{d}z\mathrm{d}r\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\norm{v(r)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}.

Also note that for any
\varepsilon>0
, the following inequality holds for
0\leq s<t\leq T
:

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\int_0^s \int_s^t (z-r)^{-1}\mathrm dz \mathrm dr}

\displaystyle=\absolutevalue{\int_0^s \ln\pr{t-r} - \ln(s - r) \mathrm dr}

\displaystyle=\absolutevalue{(t-s)\ln(t-s)}+\absolutevalue{t\ln(t) - s \ln(s)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{t-s}^{1-\frac{\varepsilon}{d_{w}}}.

Hence we can pick
0<\varepsilon=\alpha-\beta
so that

\displaystyle\norm{P_1 \int_0^s \pr{P_{t-s}^{(b)} - P_{s-t}^{(b)}} v(r) \mathrm ds}_{L^{p}_{M}}\lesssim\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\norm{v(r)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}\absolutevalue{t-s}^{\frac{d_{w}-\varepsilon}{d_{w}}}\leq\sup_{r\in[0,t]}\norm{v(r)}_{\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}}\absolutevalue{t-s}^{\frac{d_{w}-\alpha+\beta}{d_{w}}}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\norm{P_1\int_s^t P^{(b)}_{t-r} v(r)\mathrm dr}_{L^{p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{s}^{t}\norm{P_{t-r}^{(b)}}_{L^{p}_{M}\to L^{p}_{M}}\norm{P_1 v(r)}_{L^{p}_{M}}\mathrm{d}r\lesssim\absolutevalue{t-s}.

Therefore, we see by (
4.17
) that

\displaystyle\norm{\mathcal R(v)}_{\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,\infty}}

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\norm{\mathcal R(v)_t}_{\mathcal{B}_{p,\infty}^{\alpha}}+\sup_{0\leq s<t\leq T}\frac{\norm{\mathcal R(v)_t - \mathcal R(v)_s}_{\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}_{p,\infty}}}{(t-s)^{\frac{\beta-\alpha+d_{w}}{d_{w}}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\norm{v}_{\mathcal{C}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{p,\infty}},

which is the desired result.
∎

5
The Edwards-Wilkinson Equation and its Wick powers

We have now developed all the analytic machinery needed to rigorously solve (
1.1
) in the renormalized sense of
[
39
,
91
,
109
]
. In this section, we first introduce
\xi
, the space-time white noise on general metric measure spaces and
Y
, the solution to the Edwards-Wilkinson equation (
1.6
). As noted in the introduction, if
d_{h}<d_{w}
, the solution is a.s. a continuous function on
M
, but can only be understood as distributions if
d_{h}\geq d_{w}
. Finally, we will construct the Wick powers of
Y
and compute their regularity indices, while making the renormalization terms explicit.

5.1
Spacetime White Noise and Itô-Wiener Chaos Expansion

Let
\xi
be a space-time white noise on
\mathbb{R}\times M
(with respect to the reference measure
\mu
) on some probability space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{\Omega},\mathbb{P})
, which we fix from now on. That is,

\left\{\xi\left(f\right);f\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times M,\mathrm{d}t\times\mu):=\mathcal{H}\right\}

is a collection of centered Gaussian random variables so that for each
f,g\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}\times M,dt\times\mu)
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\xi(f)\xi(g)\right]=\int_{\mathbb{R}}\int_{M}f(t,x)g(t,x)\mu(\mathrm{d}x)\mathrm{d}t

We set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t}:=\sigma\left(\xi(\phi):\phi|_{(t,\infty)\times M}\equiv 0,\,\phi\in L^{2}\left(\mathbb{R}\times M,\mathrm{d}t\times\mu\right)\right),\qquad t\in\mathbb{R},

and denote
(\mathcal{F}_{t})_{t>-\infty}
the usual augmentation of the filtration
(\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{t})_{t>-\infty}.

We now assume that
\mathcal{F}_{\Omega}
is the
\sigma-
algebra generated by
\set{\xi(f)}_{f\in\mathcal{H}}
. Under this assumption, it is well known (see, for example
[
98
,
76
]
) that we have the orthogonal decomposition

L^{2}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{\Omega},\mathbb{P})=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}^{\infty}\mathcal{H}^{k},

where
\mathcal{H}^{k}
is the
k-
th homogeneous Wiener chaos. More precisely,
\mathcal{H}^{0}=\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{H}^{1}=\set{\xi(\phi):\phi\in\mathcal{H}}
and

\mathcal{H}^{k}=\set{I_{k}(\phi):\phi\in\mathcal{H}^{\otimes_{\text{sym}}k}}.

Here
\mathcal{H}^{\otimes_{\text{sym}}k}
denotes the set of square integrable, symmetric kernels
\phi:(\mathbb{R}\times M)^{k}\to\mathbb{R}
and
I_{k}
is the
k-
fold iterated stochastic integral (see
[
98
, Section 1.1.1, 1.1.2]
and
[
76
]
). We recall the following useful estimate from
[
97
]
(see also
[
98
, Theorem 1.4.1]
).

Proposition 5.1

(Nelson’s Estimate)
.

For any
k\geq 2
,
p\geq 2
, and
X\in\mathcal{H}^{k}
, we have

\norm{X}_{L^{p}_{\Omega}}^{2}\lesssim\norm{X}_{L^{2}_{\Omega}}^{2}.

5.2
The Edwards-Wilkinson Equation

We denote
Y
as the solution of the Edwards-Wilkinson equation on
\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M
with appropriate initial condition
Y_{0}
, which we will specify later,

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}Y=-(L+1)Y+\xi\\
Y(0,\cdot)=Y_{0}.\end{cases}

(5.1)

For
d_{h}\geq d_{w}
(which we assumed),
Y(t):=Y(t,\cdot)
must be understood as a distribution on
M
and is not point-wise defined for
x\in M
. Motivated by distribution theory and Duhamel’s principle, we define the solution
Y
of (
5.1
) as follows: for every test function
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}
and
t>0
,

\displaystyle Y(t,\varphi):=\left\langle e^{-t}P_{t}Y_{0},\varphi\right\rangle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{M}\left\langle\varphi,e^{-(t-s)}p_{t-s}(y,\cdot)\right\rangle\xi(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s).

(5.2)

An important case is when
Y_{0}:=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{-s}p_{-s}(\cdot,y)\xi(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s)
, then solution to (
5.1
) is a stationary (in time) Gaussian field with covariance function

\displaystyle G(x,y):=2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}p_{t}(x,y)\mathrm{d}s,\qquad x,y\in M.

That is, for any
\varphi,\psi\in\mathcal{S}
and
t\geq 0
,
Y_{t}(\varphi)
is a mean zero Gaussian random variable and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y\left(t,\varphi\right)Y\left(t,\psi\right)\right]=\iint_{M^{2}}\varphi(x)G(x,y)\psi(y)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

5.3
Wick Powers

Recall that given any
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, the
n
-th Hermite polynomial with variance
\sigma^{2}
on
\mathbb{R}
can be inductively defined by setting
H_{0}(x,\sigma^{2})=1
, and for
n\geq 1
,

\displaystyle H_{n}(x,\sigma^{2})=xH_{n-1}(x,\sigma^{2})-\sigma^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}H_{n-1}(x,\sigma^{2}).

For the rest of this paper, we will denote

\displaystyle\alpha_{0}:=\frac{d_{h}-d_{w}}{2}\geq 0.

(5.3)

and

\displaystyle n_{0}=\max\left\{n\in\mathbb{N}:n<\frac{d_{h}}{d_{h}-d_{w}}\right\}.

(5.4)

Denote
\nu
as the law of the stationary solution to (
5.1
), i.e.

\displaystyle\nu:=\mathrm{Law}\left(\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{s(L+1)}\xi\left(\mathrm{d}s\right)\right)

(5.5)

We denote

\displaystyle G_{\varepsilon}(x,y):=2\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-t}p_{t+2\varepsilon}(x,y)\mathrm{d}t,\qquad x,y\in M,

(5.6)

and
C_{\varepsilon}(x):=G_{\varepsilon}(x,x)
for
x\in M
, which will be the counter term in the Wick renormalization. By elementary computation using (

HKE
f

), we see it holds uniformly in
x\in M
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
that

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}(x)\asymp\begin{cases}1+\log\left(\varepsilon^{-1}\right),&\text{ for }d_{h}=d_{w}\\
\varepsilon^{-\frac{d_{h}-d_{w}}{d_{w}}},&\text{ for }d_{h}>d_{w}.\end{cases}

Observe that it is spatially dependent rather than a constant. For the rest of this section, we shall fix an arbitrary
\alpha>\alpha_{0}
.

Definition 5.2
.

Let
X\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty})
be a time-dependent distribution (on
M
) and
\varepsilon>0
, we define its
\varepsilon
-approximate Wick power of
n
-th order to be

\displaystyle X^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}(t,x):=H_{n}\left(X_{\varepsilon}(t,x),C_{\varepsilon}(x)\right),\qquad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M,

where
X_{\varepsilon}(t,x):=P_{\varepsilon}(X_{t})(x)
for
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M
and
H_{n}
as in the
n
-th Hermit polynomial.

Let
\mathfrak{A}\subset\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty})
be the subset of all time-dependent distributions
X
so that the collection of distributions

\displaystyle\underline{\mathbf{X}}_{n_{0}}:=\left(X,X^{:2:},\dots,X^{:n_{0}:}\right):=\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\left(X_{\varepsilon}^{:k:}\right)_{k=1}^{n_{0}}\in\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n_{0}}\mathcal{C}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha k}_{\infty}\right).

The proof of the following lemma can be found in the Appendix.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varepsilon>0
and
Y
be solution to (
5.1
) with
Y_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty}
for all sufficiently small
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
,

\displaystyle Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}\in\mathcal{C}^{\delta_{1}}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{C}^{\delta_{2}}_{M}\right)

To construct renormalized Wick powers of
Y
, we follow the approach outlined in
[
33
]
, starting with the following definition.

Definition 5.4

(
[
33
, Definition 3.11]
)
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{C}
the set of all
Y_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty}
so that the stochastic processes
\{Y^{:k:}_{t}\}_{t\in[0,1]}
for
k=1,\dots,n_{0}
converges in
\mathcal{C}([0,1];\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha k}_{\infty})
in
L^{q}_{\Omega}
for every
q\in[1,\infty)
and
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
.

The following theorem is the main result of this subsection which shows the Wick power defined above is meaningful and has a limit in an appropriate Besov space. In the following theorem, we use the convention that
\frac{a}{0}=\infty
for
a>0
.

Theorem 5.5
.

Let
Y_{0}\sim\nu
, then

\underline{\mathbf{Y}}_{n_{0}}^{\varepsilon}:=\left(Y^{:k:}_{\varepsilon}\right)_{k=1}^{n_{0}}
converges in
\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n_{0}}\mathcal{C}\left([0,T];\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha k}_{\infty}\right)
for each
T>0
. More specifically, there exists
\underline{\mathbf{Y}}_{n_{0}}=(Y,Y^{:2:},\dots,Y^{:n_{0}:})\in\bigoplus_{k=1}^{n_{0}}\mathcal{C}\left(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha k}_{\infty}\right)
so that for each
T>0
and
p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}\sim\nu}\left[\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}-Y^{:n:}}_{\mathcal{C}_{T}^{\gamma}\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha n}_{\infty}}^{p}\right]=0,\qquad 1\leq n\leq n_{0},

for all sufficiently small
\gamma>0
. In addition,
\nu(\mathfrak{C})=1
.

Note that
Y^{:1:}=Y
in Theorem
5.5
. In light of Theorem
5.5
, we
define the Wick powers
of
Y
as the limit of
Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
. We will need a few lemmas to prove it.

Remark 5.6
.

Let
Y_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty}
so that
Y\in\mathcal{C}(\mathbb{R}_{+};\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty})
is the solution to (
5.1
), then
Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}
is equal to the projection of
Y^{n}_{\varepsilon}
onto
\mathcal{H}^{n}
(c.f.
[
98
, Exercise 1.1.1]
).

The following Lemma is a consequence of semi-group property.

Lemma 5.7
.

For
u>0
and
k\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle q_{k,u}(x,y)=\int_{M}q_{k,u/2}(x,z)p_{u/2}(z,y)\mu(\mathrm{d}z).

Lemma 5.8
.

Denote
r_{0}:=\mathrm{diam}(M)

•

Suppose
a>d_{w}-d_{h}
. The following inequality holds uniformly in
y_{1},\,y_{2}\in M
with
y_{1}\neq y_{2}
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-s}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s\lesssim d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}.

•

Suppose
a=d_{w}-d_{h}
. For any
\varepsilon>0
, the following inequality holds uniformly in
t\in(0,T]
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
with
d(y_{1},y_{2})>0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-t}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s\asymp 1+\log\left(d(y_{1},y_{2})^{-1}\right).

•

For any
a\in\mathbb{R}
, the following inequality holds uniformly in
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
with
d(y_{1},y_{2})>0
,

\displaystyle d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}\lesssim\int_{0}^{1}t^{-a/d_{w}}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-t}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s

Proof.

Let
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
and denote
r:=d(y_{1},y_{2})
. Suppose first that
a>d_{w}-d_{h}
. Using the change of variable with
u=rs^{-\frac{1}{d_{w}}}
, we see that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-s}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:sgu}}{\asymp}\int_{0}^{r_{0}}s^{-(d_{h}+a)/d_{w}}\exp\left(-c\left(r/s^{1/d_{w}}\right)^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}s+1

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}\int_{r\cdot r_{0}^{-\frac{1}{d_{w}}}}^{\infty}u^{d_{h}+a-d_{w}}\exp\left(-cu^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{d_{h}+a-d_{w}}\exp\left(-cu^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a},

where we’ve used the fact that
d_{w}-d_{h}-a<0
and
0<r\leq r_{0}<\infty
.

Suppose now
a=d_{w}-d_{h}
, then by another change of variable, we see for any
T>0
and
\varepsilon>0
, the following inequality holds uniformly in
t\in(0,T]
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
with
d(y_{1},y_{2})>0
:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}s^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-s}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\int_{0}^{r^{d_{w}}}+\int_{r^{d_{w}}}^{r_{0}^{d_{w}}}\right)s^{-1}\exp\left(-c\left(r/s^{\frac{1}{d_{w}}}\right)^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}s+1

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-cu^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\frac{du}{u}+\int_{r/r_{0}}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}+1

\displaystyle\lesssim 1+\log\left(\frac{1}{r}\right).

For the last inequality, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-t}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{0}^{1}t^{-a/d_{w}}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq:sgu}}{\gtrsim}\int_{0}^{1}t^{-(a+d_{h})/d_{w}}\exp\left(-C\left(\frac{r}{t^{\frac{1}{d_{w}}}}\right)^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)dt

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}\int_{r}^{\infty}t^{d_{h}+a-d_{w}-1}\exp\left(-Ct^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}\int_{r_{0}}^{\infty}t^{d_{h}+a-d_{w}-1}\exp\left(-Ct^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-a}.

Furthermore, if
a=d_{w}-d_{h}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}t^{-a/d_{w}}e^{-t}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{0}^{r_{0}}t^{-a/d_{w}}p_{t}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{r/r_{0}}^{\infty}\exp\left(-ct^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim\int_{r/r_{0}}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}t}{t}+\int_{1}^{\infty}\exp\left(-ct^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\gtrsim 1+\log\left(\frac{1}{r}\right).

∎

Lemma 5.9
.

For
\varepsilon>0
,
i=1,2
and
(t_{i},y_{i})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M
, let

\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2}):=\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}\wedge t_{2}}e^{-(t_{1}+t_{2}-2s)}p_{t_{1}+t_{2}+2\varepsilon-2s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s.

Then it holds uniformly for
y_{1}\neq y_{2}\in M
,
0\leq t_{1},t_{2}
and
\varepsilon\in[0,1)
that

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})\lesssim\begin{cases}d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}}&\text{ if }d_{w}<d_{h}\\
1+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right)&\text{ if }d_{w}=d_{h},\end{cases}

(5.7)

where
r_{0}:=\mathrm{diam}(M)
.
For
\delta\in(0,d_{w}]
, it holds uniformly for
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
,
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
0\leq t_{1},t_{2}
that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{1},y_{1},y_{2})-\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}},

(5.8)

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2) - \mathcal K_{\varepsilon'}(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2)}

\displaystyle\lesssim d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\absolutevalue{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}.

Proof.

Let us denote
r:=d(y_{1},y_{2})
, we see by Lemma
5.8
that uniformly in
\varepsilon\in[0,1]
,
0<t_{1},t_{2}
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
with
d(y_{1},y_{2})>0
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},,y_{1},y_{2})=\frac{e^{2\varepsilon}}{2}\int_{|t_{1}-t_{2}|+2\varepsilon}^{\infty}e^{-s}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})ds\lesssim\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{-s}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})ds\lesssim\begin{cases}r^{d_{w}-d_{h}},&\text{ if }d_{h}>d_{w}\\
1+\log\left(\frac{r_{0}}{r}\right),&\text{ if }d_{h}=d_{w}\end{cases},

which gives (
5.7
).

For (
5.8
), let
\delta\in(0,d_{w})
and
\eta_{\delta}:=(d_{h}-d_{w}+\delta)\frac{d_{w}-1}{d_{w}}
. Observe that by a change of variable the following inequality holds uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
,
0<t_{1},t_{2}
and
y_{1}\neq y_{2}\in M
:

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{1},y_{1},y_{2})-\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})\right|

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{2\varepsilon}}{2}\int_{2\varepsilon}^{\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}+2\varepsilon}e^{-s}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\int_{2\varepsilon}^{\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}+2\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\exp\left(-cr^{\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}}s^{-\frac{1}{d_{w}-1}}\right)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{-\frac{d_{w}}{d_{w}-1}\eta_{\delta}}\int_{2\varepsilon}^{\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}+2\varepsilon}s^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}s^{\frac{1}{d_{w}-1}\eta_{\delta}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\int_{2\varepsilon}^{\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2 }+2\varepsilon}s^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\int_{0}^{\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2 }}s^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}.

Where we used the elementary fact that for every
p>0
,
\exp\left(-a\right)\lesssim a^{-p}
uniformly in
a>0
in the second and third line. Finally, we observe that for
1\geq\varepsilon^{\prime}>\varepsilon>0
, and
t_{2}:=t_{1}+2(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t_{1},t_{1},y_{1},y_{2})=e^{2(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}\wedge t_{2}}e^{-(t_{1}+t_{2}-2s)}p_{t_{1}+t_{2}+2\varepsilon}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s=e^{2(\varepsilon^{\prime}-\varepsilon)}\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2}).

Therefore, by the first inequality in (
5.8
), it holds uniformly in
t_{1}\geq 0
,
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
and
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2) - \mathcal K_{\varepsilon'}(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2)}

\displaystyle\leq\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2) - \mathcal K_{\varepsilon}(t_1,t_2,y_1,y_2)}

\displaystyle\qquad+\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_2, y_1,y_2)}\absolutevalue{e^{2(\varepsilon' - \varepsilon)} -1}

\displaystyle\lesssim d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\absolutevalue{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}+\absolutevalue{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_2, y_1,y_2)}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq: cal K bounds}}{\lesssim}d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\absolutevalue{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}},

where we used the fact that
d_{w}>\delta>0
and
1+\log{r_0/r}\lesssim r^{-\delta}
uniformly in
0<r\leq r_{0}
.
∎

We will also need the following elementary result, whose proof can be found in the Appendix.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
a\in[0,1)
and
b\in\mathbb{R}
, it holds uniformly in
0<u\leq 1
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{I}(u):=\int_{0}^{1}s^{-a}(u+s)^{-b}\mathrm{d}s\asymp\begin{cases}1,&\text{for }a+b<1\\
1+\log(1/u),&\text{for }a+b=1\\
u^{1-a-b},&\text{for }a+b>1\end{cases}

Lemma 5.11
.

There exists a sufficiently small
\delta>0
so that for each
p\in\mathbb{N}
, the following inequality holds uniformly in
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
and
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}\sim\nu}\left[\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1) - Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2)}_{\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha n}_{2p,2p}}^{2p}\right]^{\frac{1}{2p}}\lesssim\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}.

Consequently, for any
T>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}\sim\nu}\left[\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}}_{\mathcal{C}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha n}_{2p,2p}}^{2p}\right]<\infty.

Proof.

For
Y_{0}:=\int_{-\infty}^{0}e^{s}p_{0-s}(\cdot,y)\xi\left(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s\right)
,
Y_{\varepsilon}(s,\cdot)
is a random field given by

\displaystyle Y_{\varepsilon}(s,x)=\int_{-\infty}^{t}\int_{M}e^{-(t-s)}p_{t-s+\varepsilon}\left(x,y\right)\xi\left(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s\right),\qquad(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M.

By Fubini and Proposition
5.1
, we see for each
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
and and
k=0,1,\dots
, the following inequality holds uniformly for
t_{1},t_{2},u>0
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{Q^{(k)}_u \left(Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1) -Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2) \right)}_{L^{2p^{\prime}}_{M}}^{2p^{\prime}}\right]

(5.9)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{M}\norm{\int_M q_{k,u}(x,y)\left(Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1,y) -Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2,y) \right)\mu(\mathrm dy)}_{L^{2p^{\prime}}_{\Omega}}^{2p^{\prime}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x)

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\int_{M}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{M}q_{k,u}(x,y)\left(Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y)-Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y)\right)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{2}\right]^{p^{\prime}}\mu(\mathrm{d}x).

By Wick’s theorem, we see for
t_{1},t_{2}>0
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y_{1})Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}(t_{2},y_{2})\right]

\displaystyle=n!\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},y_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}(t_{2},y_{2})\right]^{n}

\displaystyle=n!\left(\int_{0}^{t_{1}\wedge t_{2}}\int_{M}p_{t_{1}-s+\varepsilon}(y_{1},z)p_{t_{2}-s+\varepsilon}(y_{2},z)\mu(\mathrm{d}z)\mathrm{d}s\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=n!\left(\int_{-\infty}^{t_{1}\wedge t_{2}}e^{-\left(t_{1}+t_{2}-2s\right)}p_{t_{1}+t_{2}-2s+2\varepsilon}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s\right)^{n}

\displaystyle=n!\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})^{n},

where
\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}
is as in Lemma
5.9
. By Fubini,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{M}q_{k,u}(x,y)\left(Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y)-Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y)\right)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{2}\right]

(5.10)

\displaystyle=\int_{M}\int_{M}q_{k,u}(x,y_{1})q_{k,u}(x,y_{2})\bigg(\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y_{2})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y_{2})\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y_{2})\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y_{1})Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y_{2})\right]\bigg)\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{2})

\displaystyle=n!\int_{M}\int_{M}q_{k,u}(x,y_{1})q_{k,u}(x,y_{2})\bigg(\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{1},y_{1},y_{2})^{n}-\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{2},t_{1},y_{1},y_{2})^{n}

\displaystyle+\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{2},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})^{n}-\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})^{n}\bigg)\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{2}).

Since
d_{h}\geq d_{w}
and
(M,d)
is a compact metric space, Lemma
5.9
gives for any
\delta\in(0,d_{w}]
, we have uniformly in
0\leq t_{1},\,t_{2}\leq T
,
y_{1},\,y_{2}\in M
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1]

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2)^n - \mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_2,t_1,y_1,y_2)^n}

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2) - \mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_2,t_1,y_1,y_2)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\sum_{k=1}^{n}\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2)^{k}\mathcal K_\varepsilon(t_1,t_1,y_1,y_2)^{n-k-1}}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq: cal k time holder}}{\lesssim}\absolutevalue{t_1 - t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}d(y_{1},y_{2})^{d_{w}-d_{h}-\delta}\left(\sup_{t_{1},t_{2}>0,\atop y_{1}\neq y_{2}\in M}\mathcal{K}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},t_{2},y_{1},y_{2})\right)^{n-1}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{ineq: cal K bounds}}{\lesssim}\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}d(y_{1},y_{2})^{n(d_{w}-d_{h})-\delta},

where we used the fact that
(1+\log(r_0/r))^{n}\lesssim r^{-\delta}
uniformly in
0<r\leq r_{0}<\infty
.

By Proposition
2.3
, there is some
c>0
so that uniformly in
(t,x,y)\in(0,\infty)\times M\times M
,

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{q_{k,u}(x,y)}\lesssim p_{ct}(x,y).

Now let
\delta>0
be sufficiently small so that
(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta<d_{w}
. Inserting above inequalities into (
5.10
) and use Lemma
5.8
to replace
d(y_{1},y_{2})^{-n(d_{w}-d_{h})-\delta}
by the time integral of the heat kernel to see

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{M}q_{k,u}(x,y)\left(Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{1},y)-Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t_{2},y)\right)\mu(\mathrm{d}y)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{t_1 - t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\int_{M}\int_{M}p_{cu}(x,y_{1})p_{cu}(x,y_{2})d(y_{1},y_{2})^{n(d_{w}-d_{h})-\delta}\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{t_1 - t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\int_{M}\int_{M}p_{cu}(x,y_{1})p_{cu}(x,y_{2})\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{d_{w}}}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{t_1 - t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{d_{w}}}p_{s+2cu}(x,x)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{t_1 - t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{d_{w}}}(s+u)^{-\frac{d_{h}}{d_{w}}}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}u^{\frac{-n(d_{h}-d_{w})-\delta}{d_{w}}},

where we used Lemma
5.10
and the fact that for
\delta>0
and
d_{h}\geq d_{w}\geq 1
,

\displaystyle 1<\frac{d_{h}+\delta}{d_{w}}<\frac{(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta+d_{h}}{d_{w}}.

Inserting this into (
5.9
) and use the compactness of
M
to see for each
p^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{Q^{(k)}_u \left(Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1) -Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2) \right)}_{L^{2p^{\prime}}_{M}}^{2p^{\prime}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2p^{\prime}}}\lesssim|t_{1}-t_{2}|^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}u^{-\frac{n(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{2d_{w}}}.

Now let
\alpha>\alpha_{0}
, select
\delta>0
so that
\alpha>\alpha_{0}+\delta
, we see by Minkowski’s inequality, it holds uniformly for
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1) - Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2)}_{\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha n}_{2p^{\prime},2p^{\prime}}}^{2p^{\prime}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{P_1 Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1) - P_1 Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2)}_{L^{2p^{\prime}}_{M}}^{2p^{\prime}}\right]^{\frac{1}{2p^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(u^{\frac{\alpha n}{d_{w}}}\norm{\norm{Q^{(k)}_u \pr{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_1) -Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}(t_2) }(\cdot)}_{L^{2p}_\Omega} }_{L^{2p^{\prime}}_{M}}\right)^{2p^{\prime}}\frac{\mathrm{d}s}{s}\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}\left(1+\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(u^{\frac{\alpha n}{d_{w}}}u^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}n+\delta/2}{d_{w}}}\right)^{2p^{\prime}}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}\right)^{\frac{1}{2p^{\prime}}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{t_1-t_2 }^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}.

Therefore, let
p^{\prime}
be sufficiently large and use Kolmogorov’s continuity test to see for each
T>0
, there exists
C=C(\gamma,\alpha,p^{\prime},T)>0
so that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha n}_{2p^{\prime},2p^{\prime}}}^{2p^{\prime}}\right]\lesssim C,

for all
\gamma\in\left(0,\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}-\frac{1}{2p^{\prime}}\right)
, as desired.
∎

Proof of Theorem
5.5
.

We will again assume without loss of generality that

\displaystyle Y_{0}=\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{M}e^{s}p_{s}(\cdot,y)\xi\left(\mathrm{d}y,\mathrm{d}s\right)

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and assume without loss of generality that
\varepsilon^{\prime}>\varepsilon
, denote for
(t,y)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M
that
\widetilde{Y}^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t,y):=Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}(t,y)-Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t,y)
. For
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
, denote

\displaystyle\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t,y_{1},y_{2}):=Y_{\varepsilon}^{:n:}(t,y_{1})Y_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}^{:n:}(t,y_{2}).

Note that by Wick’s Theorem, for any
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,
t\geq 0
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\Delta^{\left(n\right)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t,y_{1},y_{2})\right]=n!\left(\int_{-\infty}^{t}e^{-(t-s)}p_{2(t-s)+\varepsilon^{\prime}+\varepsilon}\mathrm{d}s\right)^{n}=n!\left(\mathcal{K}_{\frac{\varepsilon+\varepsilon^{\prime}}{2}}(t,t,y_{1},y_{2})\right)^{n},

and

\displaystyle\widetilde{Y}^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t_{1},y)-\widetilde{Y}^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t_{2},y)=Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}(t_{1},y)-Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t_{1},y)-Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}(t_{2},y)+Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t_{2},y).

Let
\delta\in(0,d_{w})
, it holds uniformly in
t\geq 0
,
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
y_{1},y_{2}\in M
that

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\E\bigg[\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}(t,y_1,y_2)-2\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t,y_1,y_2)+\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon',\varepsilon'}(t,y_1,y_2) \bigg]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle n!\absolutevalue{ \mathcal K_\varepsilon(t,t,y_1,y_2)^n - 2\mathcal K_{\frac{\varepsilon+\varepsilon'}{2}}(t,t,y_1,y_2)^n +\mathcal K_{\varepsilon'}(t,t,y_1,y_2)^n}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_{\varepsilon}(t,t,y_1,y_2) - \mathcal K_{\frac{\varepsilon+\varepsilon'}{2}}(t,t,y_1,y_2)}\absolutevalue{\sum_{k = 0}^{n-1} \mathcal K_{\varepsilon}(t,t,y_1,y_2)^k \mathcal K_{\frac{\varepsilon+\varepsilon'}{2}}(t,t,y_1,y_2)^{n-1-k}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\absolutevalue{\mathcal K_{\varepsilon'}(t,t,y_1,y_2) - \mathcal K_{\frac{\varepsilon+\varepsilon'}{2}}(t,t,y_1,y_2)}\absolutevalue{\sum_{k = 0}^{n-1} \mathcal K_{\varepsilon'}(t,t,y_1,y_2)^k \mathcal K_{\frac{\varepsilon+\varepsilon'}{2}}(t,t,y_1,y_2)^{n-1-k}}

\displaystyle\lesssim d(y_{1},y_{2})^{n(d_{w}-d_{h})-\delta}\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}},

where we used Lemma
5.9
in the last line.

Hence, for any
k=0,1,\dots
, we may follow the proof of Lemma
5.11
to see it holds uniformly for
u,\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(Q^{(k)}_{u}\left(\widetilde{Y}^{:n:}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t)\right)(x)\right)^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\int_{M^{2}}q_{k,u}(x,y_{1})q_{k,u}(x,y_{2})\mathbb{E}\bigg[\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon}(t,y_{1},y_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad-2\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t,y_{1},y_{2})+\Delta^{(n)}_{\varepsilon^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}}(t,y_{1},y_{2})\bigg]\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{M^{2}}p_{cu}(x,y_{1})p_{cu}(x,y_{2})d(y_{1},y_{2})^{n(d_{w}-d_{h})-\delta}\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}y_{2})

\displaystyle\lesssim\iint_{M^{2}}p_{cu}(x,y_{1})p_{cu}(x,y_{2})\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{d_{w}}}p_{s}(y_{1},y_{2})\mathrm{d}s\mu(y_{1})\mu(y_{2})

\displaystyle=\absolutevalue{\varepsilon-\varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{(n-1)(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{d_{w}}}p_{2cu+s}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{d_{w}}}u^{-\frac{n(d_{h}-d_{w})+\delta}{d_{w}}},

where we used Lemma
5.8
in the second line and Lemma
5.10
in the last line. Hence, by Proposition
5.1
, we see for each
p\geq 1
and
\delta\in(0,d_{w})
and
k=0,1,\dots,
it holds uniformly in
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times M
that

\displaystyle\norm{Q^{(k)}_u\pr{\widetilde Y_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}^{(n)}(t)}(x)}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}\lesssim\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}u^{-\frac{\alpha_{0}n+\delta/2}{d_{w}}}.

Hence, for any
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha_{0}
, we may select a sufficiently small
\delta>0
so that
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha_{0}+\delta
By Fubini’s, Minkowski’s inequality and Proposition
5.1
, we see it holds uniformly in
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
t\geq 0
that

\displaystyle\norm{\norm{\widetilde Y_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}^{(n)}(t)}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{ \norm{P_1\pr{\widetilde Y_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}^{(n)}(t)}(\cdot)}_{L^{2p}_\Omega} }_{L^{2p}_{M}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(u^{\frac{\alpha^{\prime}n}{d_{w}}}\norm{\norm{\pr{Q^{(k)}_u\pr{\widetilde Y_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}^{(n)}(t)}(\cdot)}}_{L^{2p}_\Omega}}_{L^{2p}_{M}}\right)^{2p}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}\left(1+\left(\int_{0}^{1}\left(u^{\frac{\alpha^{\prime}n}{d_{w}}-\frac{\alpha_{0}n-\delta/2}{2d_{w}}}\right)^{2p}\frac{\mathrm{d}u}{u}\right)^{\frac{1}{2p}}\right)\lesssim\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}.

Therefore, uniformly
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
and
t,t^{\prime}\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\norm{\norm{\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t) -\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t')}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \norm{\pr{Y^{:n:}_\varepsilon(t) - Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon'}(t)}- \pr{Y^{:n:}_\varepsilon(t') - Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon'}(t')}}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\sup_{t\geq 0}\norm{\norm{\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\epsilon,\varepsilon'}(t)}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}\lesssim\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}.

On the other hand, as we see in Lemma
5.11
,

\displaystyle\norm{\norm{\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t) -\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t')}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle=\norm{ \norm{\pr{Y^{:n:}_\varepsilon(t) - Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon'}(t)}- \pr{Y^{:n:}_\varepsilon(t') - Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon'}(t')}}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\leq\norm{\norm{Y^{:n:}_\varepsilon(t) - Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon}(t')}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}+\norm{\norm{Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon'}(t) - Y^{:n:}_{\varepsilon'}(t')}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\absolutevalue{t-t'}^{\frac{\delta}{2d_{w}}}.

This implies, for each
\alpha^{\prime}>\alpha_{0}
, there exists a sufficiently small
\delta>0
so that uniformly in
t,t^{\prime}\geq 0
and
\varepsilon,\varepsilon^{\prime}\in(0,1]
,

\displaystyle\norm{\norm{\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t) -\widetilde Y^{(n)}_{\varepsilon,\varepsilon'}(t')}_{\mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}\lesssim\absolutevalue{\varepsilon- \varepsilon'}^{\frac{\delta}{4d_{w}}}\absolutevalue{t - t'}^{\frac{\delta}{4d_{w}}}.

We may pick
p\geq 1
sufficiently large, and apply Kolmogorov’s continuity test (first to time variable and then to
\varepsilon\in(0,1]
) to see there exists
\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}>0
, so that for each
T>0

\displaystyle\norm{\sup_{0< \varepsilon< \varepsilon' \le 1}\abs{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{-\gamma'}\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:} - Y_{\varepsilon'}^{:n:}}_{\mathcal C^\gamma_T \mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}<\infty.

Finally, we may choose
\alpha^{\prime}\in(\alpha_{0},\alpha)
, and choose
p\geq 1
sufficiently large so that
\alpha^{\prime}+d_{h}/p<\alpha
. Then by Proposition
3.11
, we see

\displaystyle\norm{\sup_{0< \varepsilon< \varepsilon' \le 1}\abs{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{-\gamma'}\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:} - Y_{\varepsilon'}^{:n:}}_{\mathcal C^\gamma_T \Binf{-n \alpha}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\norm{\sup_{0< \varepsilon< \varepsilon' \le 1}\abs{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{-\gamma'}\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:} - Y_{\varepsilon'}^{:n:}}_{\mathcal C^\gamma_T \Binf{-n \alpha' - d_h/2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\norm{\sup_{0< \varepsilon< \varepsilon' \le 1}\abs{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{-\gamma'}\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:} - Y_{\varepsilon'}^{:n:}}_{\mathcal C^\gamma_T \mathcal B^{-\alpha' n}_{2p,2p}}}_{L^{2p}_{\Omega}}<\infty.

In particular, this implies for each
p\geq 1
and
T>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}\sim\nu}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sup_{0<\varepsilon<\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq 1}\absolutevalue{\varepsilon' - \varepsilon}^{-\gamma^{\prime}}\norm{Y_\varepsilon^{:n:} - Y_{\varepsilon'}^{:n:}}_{\mathcal{C}^{\gamma}_{T}\mathcal{B}^{-n\alpha}_{\infty}}\right)^{2p}|Y_{0}\right]\right],

which gives the desired result.
∎

We list some consequences of Theorem
5.5
. Recall the binomial identity for Hermite polynomials: for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle H_{n}(x+y,\sigma^{2})=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}x^{k}H_{n-k}(y,\sigma^{2}),\qquad x,y,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}.

(5.11)

The lemma below is similar to that of
[
33
, Lemma 3.6]
.

Lemma 5.12
.

Suppose
1\leq n\leq n_{0}
and
\alpha>\alpha_{0}
satisfies
\alpha(n-1)<\Theta
, where
\Theta\in(0,1]
is as in (

HC
Θ

). Let
Y
be solution to (
5.1
) with initial condition
Y_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}
and
\gamma\in(\alpha(n-1),\Theta)
. Then for any
v\in\mathcal{C}([0,1];\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty})
,

\displaystyle\left(\left(v_{t}+Y_{t}\right)^{:n:}\right)_{t\in[0,1]}=\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}v^{k}_{t}Y^{:n-k:}_{t}\right)_{t\in[0,1]}\in\mathcal{C}\left([0,1];\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha n}_{\infty}\right).

Moreover, almost surely, the map

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\left([0,1];\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}\right)\ni v\mapsto\left(v+Y\right)^{:n:}

is locally Lipschitz and polynomial of degree
n
.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
1.7
and Proposition
3.7
, where
\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}
is a Banach algebra and (
5.11
).
∎

As in
[
33
, Lemma 3.13]
, we show
\mathfrak{C}
is stable under addition of sufficiently smooth functions. We denote for
\Psi_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty}
that

\displaystyle\llbracket\Psi_{0}\rrbracket_{p}:=\max_{k=1,\dots,n_{0}}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}=\Psi_{0}}\left[\norm{Y^{:k:}}_{\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha k}_{\infty}}^{p}\right]^{\frac{1}{p}}

Lemma 5.13
.

Take
\alpha>\alpha_{0}
and
1\leq n\leq n_{0}
be as in Lemma
5.12
. Let
\Psi_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}
and
v_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}
with
\gamma>\alpha(n-1)
. Then
\Psi_{0}+v_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}
and

\displaystyle\llbracket\Psi_{0}+v_{0}\rrbracket_{p}\lesssim\left(1+\llbracket\Psi_{0}\rrbracket_{p}\right)\left(1+\norm{v_0}_{\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}}\right)^{n_{0}}.

Proof.

By Lemma
3.5
, the map
t\mapsto e^{-(1+L)t}v_{0}
is in
\mathcal{C}\left([0,1];\mathcal{B}^{\beta}_{\infty}\right)
for any
\beta\in(\alpha(n-1),\gamma)
. Hence the desired result follows from Lemma
5.12
.
∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Under the settings of Lemma
5.12
, let
\Psi_{0}\in\mathfrak{C}
and let
Y
solve (
5.1
) with
Y(0,\cdot)=\Psi_{0}
. Then, for every
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and every
q\in[1,\infty)
,

\sup_{t\geq 0}\mathbb{E}_{Y_{0}=\Psi_{0}}\left[\norm{Y^{:k:}}_{\mathcal{C}([t,t+1];\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha k}_{\infty})}^{q}\right]<\infty.

Proof.

Fix
k\in\{1,\dots,n\}
and
q\in[1,\infty)
. Let
\widetilde{Y}_{0}\sim\nu
be independent of
\xi
, and let
\widetilde{Y}
be the solution to (
5.1
) driven by the same noise
\xi
with initial condition
\widetilde{Y}(0,\cdot)=\widetilde{Y}_{0}
. For each
t\geq 0
and
s\in[0,1]
, set

\displaystyle v^{(t)}_{s}:=e^{-(1+L)(t+s)}(\Psi_{0}-\widetilde{Y}_{0}),

so that
Y(t+s)=\widetilde{Y}(t+s)+v^{(t)}_{s}
.

Choose
\gamma\in(\alpha(n-1),\Theta)
. By Lemma
3.5
, we have for
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\norm{v^{(t)}}_{\mathcal{C}_{1}\mathcal{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty}}\lesssim\norm{\Psi_0-\widetilde Y_0}_{\mathcal{B}^{-\alpha}_{\infty}}.

Hence for every
r\geq 1
,
